TIER: T2
REGIONS: dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166, dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167
SHARED_REDUCTION: dsp_block_16_8_false.resulta_tmp (the 24-bit sum ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin, read out as resulta)
BLOCKS: 72
TILES: 150
STAGE_NS: 5.62
MAPPING: per dsp_block_16_8_false instance, one mult_add_int_18x19:
clk ← clk; reset ← aclr (the only reset of ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg/by_reg/resulta_tmp, synchronous, active high, clears to 0; the module's ena port is unused in the baseline body, so no enable is lost); mode_sigs ← 12'b0; scanin ← 19'b0, scanout left unconnected.
ax[17:0] ← {2'b0, ax[15:0]} (region $1166's first operand, 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended, max 65535 < 2^17).
ay[18:0] ← {11'b0, ay[7:0]} (region $1166's second operand, 8-bit unsigned, zero-extended).
bx[35:0] ← {12'b0, prod_b[23:0]} where wire [23:0] prod_b = bx[15:0] * by[7:0]; — region $1167's product, left as a plain * so it maps to a hard 18x19 multiply (two per tile, exactly as in the baseline), combinational into the block's addend port; unsigned, max 16,711,425 < 2^24, zero-extended.
chainin[63:0] ← the module's chainin port, which the single instantiation (dot_product_16_8_30_2, line 11687) ties to 64'd0 — a constant driver, the only kind this dedicated pin accepts, and it is the term the baseline adds at the result register.
result[63:0] → assign resulta = result[23:0]; and assign chainout = {40'b0, result[23:0]}; (wires and constants only).

PLAN:

What the block computes, and why it is bit-exact. The baseline module (lines 11727–11749) registers ax,ay,bx,by at edge k and registers ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin into the 24-bit resulta_tmp at edge k+1. The block's own two register stages stand exactly in place of those: its operand register replaces ax_reg, ay_reg (driven directly) and replaces bx_reg/by_reg by registering their product instead of their operands — equivalent because the multiply is combinational, so the value registered on bx at edge k is bx@k * by@k, and because aclr clearing bx_reg/by_reg to 0 and clearing the bx port register to 0 both contribute 0 to the sum. Its result register replaces resulta_tmp. Module latency is 2 cycles before and 2 cycles after; nothing else moves. All operands are unsigned and narrower than their signed ports, so zero-extension is exact; the full sum ax*ay + bx*by + chainin can exceed 2^24 and the baseline's 24-bit register wraps — taking result[23:0] reproduces that wrap bit for bit, and truncation commutes with the +chainin term, so correctness does not depend on chainin being 0. Nothing is truncated before the sum in this region, so the fusion is legal here.

Live values preserved. resulta_tmp is read only by resulta and chainout; ax_reg…by_reg are read only by the sum. resulta drives o_result → DP_res_* → accumulator_24_30_3.i_result, which sign-extends bit 23 — untouched, since resulta carries the same bits on the same cycles. chainout is rebuilt as {40'b0, result[23:0]} rather than taken from the block's chainout pin, so its upper 40 bits stay 0 as the baseline has them (chains_0 in dot_product_16_8_30_2 is declared and never read, but the port is preserved anyway). No new operation or datatype family: the only cells added are the block and the wiring; the bx*by multiply is the baseline's own expression, unmoved.

Blocks, tiles, stages. 72 blocks (one per dsp_block_16_8_false instance; the module is instantiated once in dot_product_16_8_30_2, 6 per processing_element, 72 in all), each alone on a tile. The 72 surviving bx*by multiplies pack two to a tile as they do today, ≈36 tiles, and the ≈84 remaining multiplies elsewhere keep the ≈42 tiles the previous build measured: ≈150 tiles, +36 over the baseline's 114, of the device's 306. (The previous build's tile count came in 2 above the estimate; expect ±2 here for the same packing reason.) No stage carries two block delays: the mult_add is a registered block, so its bx input pin ends the stage that the combinational multiply sits on, and its own 2.14 ns runs between its two internal registers. The stage I create is register → wire → hard multiply (2.14) → wire → block operand pin: 0.06 + 2.25 + 2.14 + 1.17 = 5.62 ns with table medians, and 5.10 ns using the 1.73 ns register-to-multiply wire the baseline timing report measured in this very region — below the baseline's 6.81 ns slowest stage either way.

What leaves the slowest stage. The baseline's slowest stage is exactly this region: multiply → 12-carry-stage soft adder (0.96) → LUT (1.39) → resulta_tmp. The adder chain, the LUT and the +chainin add all move inside the block, where rule 4 makes them free. This is the design's slowest stage and its second-slowest (same shape, a different instance of the same module), and since every one of the 72 instances is that same shape, all 72 must be converted — converting a subset would leave 6.81 ns standing and buy only tiles. The previous build's 5.98771 ns path ran from a flip-flop through four LUT levels into a soft ALU bit inside a converted dsp_block (…cell_hard_block$27364.…replace_alu$57355.Y[26]) and straight on into the accumulator's carry chain: that soft 24-bit adder was the price of putting the two products in two separate blocks and summing them outside. This plan has no soft arithmetic left in dsp_block_16_8_false at all, so that path disappears, 72 blocks and ~36 tiles are saved against the previous build, and the expected product is ≈1.68e+08 × ≈5.6–6.0 ns ≈ 0.94–1.01e+09, i.e. −12% to −18% against the baseline's 1.14185e+09 and below the kept 1.0693e+09 on area alone even if the period only matches 5.99 ns.

Candidates left, and why. All 16 winograd_dsp_16 candidates (both $733/$734 products, both sum_in=winograd_transform_0 and =winograd_transform_1, all #1–#4 shapes) are left, for one shared set of reasons: (a) each product is truncated into a 16-bit register (resa_reg <= ay_reg * coefa, lines 8966–8967) and the parent's winograd_adder_16_20_4 tree zero-extends that truncated 16-bit value into 20-bit adds — the design truncates each product before the sum, which the table forbids fusing; (b) the coefficient operand is a 16-bit register holding a negative constant encoded unsigned (−5 as 65531, etc.), so on a signed 19-bit port only the low 16 bits of the product agree with the design, which is the same truncation requirement again; (c) the products leave their module on resulta/resultb and several feed more than one reduction (dsp_out_1_4 goes to both winograd_adder_16_20_4_WA00.data7x and …_WA10.data10x), so they feed separate sums and are observable at ports; (d) their sums sit three pipeline levels deep in another module, so fusing would change where registers sit across a module boundary. On top of that, they are not on the slowest stage, so blocks spent there would be paid for in tiles alone (≈9 CLBs each) with no period to show for it.

Risks. (1) The surviving bx * by must still be inferred onto a DSP tile; it is the baseline's own expression with the same widths in the same module, but if the flow mapped it to soft logic the new stage would blow past 6.81 ns. (2) The register-to-multiply and multiply-to-block wires are the sampled ranges in the table; at their high ends (3.82 + 2.22) this stage would reach 8.2 ns, though the baseline measured 1.73 ns on the first of them in this exact region. (3) The period may be floored by a soft path outside this region near 6 ns, in which case the gain is the 36 tiles and the removed soft adder rather than more speed — still a lower product than the kept result.

Read these files in this directory: dla_like.small.v

response

APPROVE

Verification against the source (dla_like.small.v:11696–11752) and the four violation criteria:

(a) Function preserved. Baseline at edge k registers ax,ay,bx,by; at edge k+1 registers ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin into 24-bit resulta_tmp (line 11745). The plan's mapping registers ax@k, ay@k, and (bx*by)@k in the block's operand stage and sums at edge k+1 — hoisting the combinational bx*by across the operand register is value-identical, and aclr clearing either bx_reg/by_reg or the bx port register yields the same zero addend. Latency stays 2 cycles, no enable is lost (ena is in the port list but never referenced in the always block). All four operands are unsigned Verilog vectors, so zero-extension into the signed 18/19/36-bit ports is exact; taking result[23:0] reproduces the baseline's 24-bit wrap, and truncation commutes with the +chainin term as claimed.

(b) No new op/datatype family. The only surviving arithmetic is the baseline's own bx*by with unchanged widths in the same module; no new operation or datatype is introduced.

(c) No fabricated terms. Both regions $1166 and $1167 are real products of line 11745 and share one reduction, resulta_tmp. One drives the 18x19 multiply, the other feeds the 36-bit addend port — both terms of the same existing sum. Nothing invented to fill n_terms.

(d) No mode island. chainin ← the module port, tied to 64'd0 at the single instantiation (line 11687) — a constant driver, which the tie-off contract explicitly permits for this dedicated pin. scanin = 0 with scanout unconnected, mode_sigs = 0, both as the contract requires. The output is live: result[23:0] → resulta → o_result (line 11692) → the accumulator that sign-extends bit 23. chainout is rebuilt as {40'b0, result[23:0]}, matching line 11749.

Structural counts check out: 12 processing_element × 6 dot_product_16_8_30_2 = 72 dsp_block_16_8_false instances, consistent with the plan's 72 blocks and with the baseline's 114 tiles (72 from this module's 144 multiplies, ~42 elsewhere). 150 tiles is within the device's 306.

The Winograd exclusions rest on a stated pre-sum truncation into 16-bit registers and multi-consumer products — a conservative call, which the tier permits. Timing and area projections carry acknowledged risk (wire-delay spread, soft-logic floor near 6 ns), but estimate quality is not a revision ground under the stated criteria.

Edit dla_like.small.v in this directory. Local restructuring: re-express the region (e.g. a multiply array + adder tree as a fused sum-of-products) to reach the mode; preserve the region's function and role, keep the rest of the design unchanged, wire the mode's real consumers.

Goal: rewrite the RTL so the hard-block mode 'mult_add_mode_18_19_36' is exercised in the packed netlist,
doing real work, while preserving the design's behaviour and role.
Tier: T2

Hard-block primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
computes: out = ax*ay + bx
input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. An unsigned design operand as wide as its port has no exact representation on this block.
timing: result is registered inside the block on clk; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
registers: the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register.
reset: reset is synchronous and active high; it clears the block's operand registers and its result register to 0.
tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it, and it is added to the sum at the result register, one clock edge before result); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
Wire EVERY operand of the fused region onto the input ports (name them exactly as above); drive the region's real consumer from the output port. Tie unused term ports to 0. Do not leave input ports unconnected.

Regions selected by the approved plan: 2 — dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166, dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167. They already feed the common reduction 'dsp_block_16_8_false.resulta_tmp (the 24-bit sum ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin, read out as resulta)'. Each 'mult_add_mode_18_19_36' block fuses 1 product term(s); the plan below states how the 2 regions map onto block instances. Fuse only these existing terms; do not invent new ones. In the design the products are registered 0 stage(s) before their sum. The sum these products feed also adds 1 term(s) that are not multiplies in the elaborated design: chainin. Every such term the approved plan does not place on a block port stays in the expression, added to the block's result where the original added it. Keep it also when it is a constant, or is tied to a constant where the module is instantiated: synthesis removes an add of a constant. Leave one out only if the approved plan says to and gives the reason, and then write that reason in a comment on the line you changed. The block is registered: its result is valid 2 cycle(s) after its operands, on the registers the contract describes. The module's latency must come out unchanged. Remove from the design exactly the register stages the approved plan says the block's own registers replace, add a delay only where the plan names one, and connect the block's clock and reset as the plan states.
Rules: edit Verilog source only; preserve the module's live inputs/outputs and its downstream
consumers; do not introduce operations or datatypes absent from the design unless this tier
admits a variant; keep the surrounding design unchanged for T1/T2. Do not fabricate terms merely
to match a mode's n_terms: a term the design did not compute is not a correct replacement.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: syntax + elaboration + cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Runs plain yosys (read_verilog; hierarchy; proc; opt; stat) on the one file: no arch, no
# parmys, no hard-block library. Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole
# design (the last "Number of cells" table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub,
# registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated
# module by name, which is where a hard-block instantiation such as comb_mult_add_16 shows up
# with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
out="$(yosys -p "read_verilog $f; hierarchy -auto-top; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi

echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"

Cost a client-side list-price estimate per call, summed, not a bill. Token components are kept separate (uncached input, output, reasoning, cache read, cache write); total is their sum, with reasoning counted once. Reasoning tokens are already counted inside the output column.

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed
5
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 1
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain -8.33% against first, min-gain 0.0492 (baseline cpd_seed_spread_frac))
baseline
layout koios_small · used area 1.67592e+08 · critical path 6.81328 ns · area x delay 1.14185e+09 (read from entry)

The objective is area x delay: VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path. Period and area are each the median over the loop's seeds; the product is median area x median period. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 4.92%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 4.92%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 5 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period and area over the seeds. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.
